TỔNG HỢP CÂU HỎI TỪ ĐỀ THI THẬT - CRAFT AND STRUCTURE


Question Section
Question 1 Easy
The following text is from John Matheus's 1925 novel Fog. The story happens in a city where heavy fog has set in. 

The little conductor stood on tiptoe in an effort to keep one hand on the signal rope, craning his neck in a vain and dissatisfied endeavor to pierce the miasma of the fog. The motorman chafed in his box, thinking of the drudging lot of the laboring man. He registered discontent.

As used in the text, what does the word "registered" most nearly mean? 
A) enrolled 
B) valued 
C) disregarded 
D) displayed 


Question 2 Easy
The following text is adapted from Anton Chekhov's 1904 play The Cherry Orchard (translated by Julius West in 1916). 

TROFIMOV: Believe me, Anya, believe me! I'm not thirty yet, I'm young. I'm still a   student, but I have undergone a great deal! I'm as hungry as the winter, I'm ill, I'm shaken....and where haven't I been fate has tossed me everywhere! 

As used in the text, what does the word "undergone" most nearly mean? 
A) Neglected 
B) Enjoyed 
C) Conveyed 
D) Endured 


Question 3 Easy
The following text is adapted from Anton Chekhov's 1904 play The Cherry Orchard (translated by Julius West in 1916). 

TROFIMOV: Believe me, Anya, believe me! I'm not thirty yet, I'm young. I'm still a   student, but I have undergone a great deal! I'm as hungry as the winter, I'm ill, I'm shaken....and where haven't I been fate has tossed me everywhere!

A) Uncovered 
B) Ingnoreed 
C) Experienced
D) Conveyed

Question 4 Easy
With his widely celebrated portraits of poet Seamus Heaney, molecular biologist Struther Arnott, and other prominent figures in different fields, British painter Peter Edwards has_____ substantial prestige as an artist. 

Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase? 
A) required 
B) remembered 
C) achieved 
D) avoided


Question 5 Easy
The following text is adapted from Kenneth Grahame's 1908 novel The Wind in the Willows. The Mole is dazed after briefly meeting a stranger while traveling with a friend. 

[The] Mole stood still a moment, held in thought. As one wakened suddenly from a beautiful dream, who struggles to recall it, and can re-capture nothing but a dim sense of the beauty of it, the beauty! Till that, too, fades away in its turn. 

As used in the text, what does the word "suddenly" most nearly mean? 
A) Abruptly 
B) Repeatedly
C) Voluntarily
D) Tenderly

From sculptures like Miró's Chicago by Joan Miró, found at Brunswick Plaza, to street art like Justus Roe's mural South Shore on South Exchange Avenue, Chicago offers an array of works to_____the tastes of art lovers.

A) supplant
B) mitigate
C) venerate
D) satiate


Question 7 Easy
The following text is adapted from William Wordsworth's 1798 poem "Lines Written a Few Miles above Tintern Abbey." 

                                          Once again 
                 Do I behold these steep and lofty cliffs, 
               Which on a wild secluded scene impress 
     Thoughts of more deep seclusion; and connect 
                 The landscape with the quiet of the sky. 

As used in the text, what does the word "deep" most nearly mean? 
A) Tough 
B) Hideous 
C) Complicated 
D) Intense
Question 8 Easy

B) requirement for
C) complaint about
D) question about

Question 10 Easy
One way to_____ the importance of a scholar's research is to track how often other scholars refer to that research. For example, University of California-Berkeley economist Ulrike Malmendier, who studies behavioral economics, is among the world's most frequently cited researchers in her field, indicating that her work has been quite significant.

A) assess
B) utilize
C) influence
D) undermine 


Question 11 Easy
Dragon against Tiger is an important work of Nihonga, or classical Japanese painting. Unlike Wada Eisaku, who adopted traditional European methods such as painting with oil on canvas, Hashimoto Gaho_____traditional Japanese approaches. For instance, Hashimoto produced Dragon against Tiger by applying color pigments to a silk surface. 

Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
A) overlooked
B) distrusted
C) embraced
D) released


Question 12 Easy
Domesticated thousands of years ago in South America, the tomatillo deviates structurally from the wild plant it is descended from. Summer squash, another domesticated crop from the Americas, doesn't closely resemble any wild plant, and genetic research only recently_____ its ancestor to be the wild Johnny gourd.

Which choice completes the text with the most logical and precise word or phrase?
A) revealed
B) expanded
C) encouraged
D) petitioned

     Tom appeared on the sidewalk with a bucket of whitewash and a long-handled brush. He surveyed the fence, and all gladness left him and a deep melancholy settled down upon his spirit. Thirty yards of board fence nine feet high.

C) Adapted
D) Examined


Question 18 Easy
The following text is from Rudolfo Anaya's 1972 novel Bless Me, Utma. The young narrator is eating breakfast with his two sisters in their home in rural New Mexico. 

I sat across the table from Deborah and Teresa and ate my atole and the hot tortilla with butter. I said very little. I usually spoke very little to my two sisters. They were older than I and they were very close. They usually spent the entire day in the attic, playing dolls and giggling. I did not concern myself with those things.

As used in the text, what does the phrase "concern myself with" most nearly mean?
A) craft
B) get angry about
C) care about
D) earn 


Question 19 Easy
The following text is from Mark Oshiro's 2018 novel Anger Is a Gift. In the novel, Moss and his friends are on a subway train in Northern California. 

    Lights from the outside world then filled the train car as it rose out of the ground and climbed the elevated track. As long as Moss had lived in West Oakland, he'd never tired of this specific view, so he pointed toward the windows. "Check it." he said, and the Port of Oakland began to pass by them.

As used in the text, what does the word "specific" most nearly mean?
A) imaginary
B) correct
C) energetic
D) particular


Question 20 Easy
The following text is adapted from John Matheus's 1925 short story "Fog." 

  The fog extended its tentacles over the city and river, gradually obliterating traces of familiar landscapes. At five-thirty the old Panhandle bridge, supported by massive sandstone pillars, stalwarts, as when erected fifty years before to serve a generation now passed behind the portals of life, had become a spectral outline against the sky.

As used in the text, what does the word "supported" most nearly mean?
A) cherished
B) defended
C) help up
D) improved on

Question 29 Easy

Mr. French, the senior partner, who sat opposite Kirby, was an older man — a safe guess would have placed him somewhere in the debatable ground between forty and fifty; of a good height, as could be seen even from the seated figure, the upper part of which was held erect with the unconscious ease which one associates with military training.

Question 32 Easy

Question 43 Easy
The following text is adapted from John Matheus’s 1926 short story, “Mr. Bradford Teaches Sunday School”. Mr. Bradford is driving through the countryside in Florida.
     The moss in the towering water oaks had become enlivened with a verdant sheen of silver and hung like festoons of carnival or like funeral decorations for the mourning of the dead. The pine green was resplendent. The bald cypresses spread themselves along the water courses while the willows wept as they always did. 

Mr. Bradford was conscious of this gorgeous display of nature.

A) reproduction
B) concealment
C) pretentiousness
D) exhibition

C) an objective
D) a repudiation

Question 64 Easy

Many researchers once thought the same about cuttlefish, but Trevor Wardill and his team wanted to test whether this assumption was true. The team studied how cuttlefish wearing 3D glasses reacted to 3D images of shrimp (a favorite prey) projected on a tank wall. Cuttlefish changed their striking position to match the 3D images, suggesting that their vision is more like that of falcons and horses than that of octopuses or squid.

Question 76 Easy
Researchers have long hypothesized that woolly mammoths were hunted to extinction in North America by humans using spears with grooved tips known as Clovis points. One anthropologist set out to test this hypothesis. Using a mechanical spear-thrower, he launched spears with Clovis points into mounds of clay-substitutes for the animals' large bodies. The projectiles generally penetrated only a few inches into the clay, an amount insufficient to have harmed most woolly mammoths. This led the anthropologist to conclude that hunters using spears with Clovis points likely weren't the principal drivers of the extinction.

Question 77 Easy
Why do rusty-spotted cats purr but jaguars roar? Researchers hypothesize that this difference between the two feline species may be partly due to a U-shaped bone in their throats called the hyoid. Rusty-spotted cats, which are much smaller than jaguars, have a rigid hyoid that rumbles when the cat's larynx vibrates, resulting in a purr. By contrast, jaguars have a somewhat flexible hyoid, and the bone is attached to the skull with a stretchy ligament that rusty-spotted cats lack. These traits allow jaguars and most other species of big cats to produce powerful roars. The same traits may also prevent most big cats from purring.

Which choice best describes the overall structure of the text?
A) The text compares the habitats of two species, then explains how those habitats are changing.
B) The text presents a theory about two species, then discusses facts that weaken it.
C) The text poses a question about two species, then presents a possible answer.
D) The text describes a behavior shared by two species, then discusses other behaviors shared by them.

In this case, the element "ho" in hopánki gets repeated in hopanhoki. There are many examples of this type of reduplication in Muscogee.

Question 107 Easy

By contrast, jaguars have a somewhat flexible hyoid, and the bone is attached to the skull with a stretchy ligament that ocelots lack. These traits allow jaguars and most other species of big cats to produce powerful roars. The same traits may also prevent most big cats from purring.

Question 195 Medium
Text 1 


Attempts to automate classification of music into genres have not been very successful, and we may be at the limit of what is technologically possible. Though many other nocturnal mammals respond to lunar intensity variations similarly to greater Egyptian gerbils, Mohol bushbabies (Galago moholi) display the opposite pattern, as their heavy reliance on visual foraging results in a different balance of reward and risk.

C) It describes a behavioral pattern displayed by Mohol bushbabies that is inconsistent with how they would be expected to respond to high lunar intensity based on benefits and costs.
D) It presents a trait of Mohol bushbabies that serves as evidence for the assertion that greater Egyptian gerbils are not the only nocturnal mammals whose activity is inhibited by high lunar intensity.


Question 224 Medium

And while no one would call the humpback whale small—it can have a mass as high as 30,000 kg—it is one of the smaller baleen whales and is much smaller than the northern right whale, which can weigh a whopping 80.000 kg and consume as much as 24,000 kg of krill per day.

D) It details a relationship between humpback and northern right whales and krill and then discusses an unexpected consequence of a change in baleen whale populations.

C) It marks a shift from a discussion of the researchers’ conclusion to a discussion of their methods.
D) It presents a potential explanation for the team’s findings that the text goes on to refute.
Question 259 Difficult
Text 1

In separate studies, Joanna Weremijewicz and Hualiang Zhang and colleagues examined whether plants transfer nutrients to one another using a common mycorrhizal network (CMN)—a lattice of fungal strands in the soil. Weremijewicz and colleagues excluded all pathways other than the CMN by using barriers to keep the plants' root systems separate while allowing mycorrhizal strands through—a crucial step Zhang and colleagues' study did not take.

Text 2

Weremijewicz and colleagues took the necessary precaution of separating the plants' root systems (thereby excluding root-to-root transmission). However, any barrier used must allow the thread-like hyphae of a CMN to pass through, and this permeability would also allow liquids through. Thus, the researchers' experimental design cannot ensure that any nutrient transfer observed can be attributed to a CMN and not to some other pathway.

Based on the texts, the author of Text 1 and the author of Text 2 would most likely give the same answer to which question?
A) Do plants that transfer nutrients through a root-to-root pathway also transmit nutrients via a CMN?
B) Is there an available barrier material that can block roots and liquids while allowing fungal strands through? 
C) Did Zhang and colleagues' study effectively exclude any nutrient pathway other than a CMN?
D) Are the barriers used in Weremijewicz and colleagues' study sufficient to ensure that nutrient transfer could only occur via a CMN?


Question 260 Difficult
Text 1

The poet Audre Lorde once claimed that poetry is the most inexpensive of art forms to practice. While people who pursue other art forms-painting, architecture, theater-require large blocks of uninterrupted time as well as money to complete their work, poets can write, as Lorde said, "between shifts, in the hospital pantry, on the subway, and on scraps of surplus paper." So poets can practice their art even if they must earn their living in another way.

Text 2

Any assessment of the state of contemporary poetry must reckon with the professionalization of the field. While it is possible in theory for anyone to publish in Virginia Quarterly Review, Kenyon Review, or a similar major poetry outlet, many people who do so have professional training in poetry and extensive practice writing it, which requires time not often available to those who must also work full-time jobs. Thus, financial security indirectly affects which people become poets.

Based on the texts, Lorde (Text 1) and the author of Text 2 would most likely disagree about the answer to which of the following questions?
A) Do other art forms than poetry require substantial investments of time and money?
B) Should poets try to gain experience working in nonartistic fields as a way of improving their poetry?
C) Does professional training in poetry affect the quality of poets’ work?
D) Is working a full-time job a major impediment to practicing the art of poetry?


Question 261 Difficult
The following text is from George Eliot's 1857 short story "The Sad Fortunes of the Rev. Amos Barton." In the text, the narrator addresses the reader directly and alludes to a discussion among Rev. Amos Barton's neighbors.

It was happy for the Rev. Amos Barton that he did not, like us, overhear the conversation recorded in the last chapter. Indeed, what mortal is there of us, who would find his satisfaction enhanced by an opportunity of comparing the picture he presents to himself of his own doings, with the picture they make on the mental retina of his neighbours? We are poor plants buoyed up by the air-vessels of our own conceit: alas for us, if we get a few pinches that empty us of that windy self-subsistence! The very capacity for good would go out of us.

Which choice best states the main idea of the text?
A) Although people seek to be viewed as virtuous, the most insignificant setbacks will often inhibit them from being so.
B) People tend to fixate more often than they should on whether their acquaintances think highly of them.
C) People are better off not knowing about the discrepancy between their own self-image and what others think of them.
D) Although people appreciate the value of being truthful, they are generally unwilling to point out others' failings.

Detailed Answer & Explanation
Question 1 Easy
The correct answer is D.
In the context of the sentence, "He registered discontent," the word "registered" means that the motorman displayed or showed his discontent.

A) enrolled: This means to sign up or enter in a list, which doesn't fit the context of showing emotion.
B) valued: This refers to considering something important, which doesn't align with expressing discontent.
C) disregarded: This means to ignore, which is the opposite of what the sentence conveys.

⇒ Therefore, "displayed" accurately captures the meaning of "registered" in this context, indicating that the motorman was visibly showing his dissatisfaction.

B) Enjoyed: This implies taking pleasure in something, which contrasts with the idea of having endured hardships.
C) Conveyed: This means to communicate or transmit something, which doesn't align with the meaning of experiencing challenges.

⇒ Therefore, "endured" best reflects the meaning of "undergone" in this context, indicating that the speaker has faced and persisted through significant struggles.

This accumulation gradually forms new portions of land, shaping the delta.

Option D) "thrive" is incorrect because thrive means to grow vigorously, which is a term typically used for living organisms. Sediments are non-living materials; they do not "thrive." While the delta's ecosystem might thrive, the sediments themselves simply pile up.

If the sentence used "lifetime misunderstandings of the arts," it would suggest that Mary Holiday Black had a flawed or incorrect grasp of the arts, which is the opposite of being honored for her excellence in the field. Thus, this option does not fit the context.
Question 63 Easy
The correct answer is A.
A) "Marks": Marks refers to symbols, signs, or identifiers placed on something. In the context of the sentence, Gogol is actively creating identifiers of his name by carving and leaving his signature. "Carves his name with paper clips" and "leaves his signature" both involve making distinct marks on his artwork. Therefore, "marks" accurately captures the meaning of both "carves" and "leaves" in this context.
B) "Passes": Passes means to move past something or to transfer something to someone. This does not fit the context of creating identifiers on his artwork. Gogol is not passing anything; he is making marks.
C) "Turns": Turns refers to moving in a different direction or rotating. This is unrelated to the act of creating marks or signatures on his art pieces. Thus, "turns" does not align with the intended meaning.
D) "Follows": Follows means to come after or to adhere to something. In this context, Gogol is not following something but rather creating his own identifiers. Therefore, "follows" is not appropriate.
Question 63’ Easy
The correct answer is A) imparts.
In the poem, the speaker says her husband's name "breathes bravery and life And courage to the tribe." Here, the word "breathes" is used metaphorically to mean that his name instills or gives these qualities to his people.
Therefore, imparts, which means to grant or bestow a quality, is the best synonym. The chief's name is described as a source that gives courage and vitality to the tribe.
The other options do not fit the context:
B) Renounces means to give up or reject, which is the opposite of the intended meaning
C) Assents means to agree, which doesn't make sense in this context.
D) Absorbs means to take in, which is also the opposite of giving something to the tribe.

The text emphasizes that this collaborative approach has built trust between government and tribal groups, thereby improving the quality of joint conservation projects.
* Text 2 reinforces this by stating that state and federal natural resources agencies are increasingly incorporating traditional ecological knowledge from tribal groups. This integration has not only strengthened positive relationships but also led to better conservation outcomes.
Both texts underscore the benefits of collaboration between tribal and government entities in conservation efforts, directly aligning with Option A.

Incorrect. The texts do not suggest that collaboration directly influences or reduces temperature increases. Instead, they focus on how collaboration improves the effectiveness and outcomes of conservation projects in response to environmental threats like rising temperatures.

Incorrect. The emphasis is on collaborative efforts improving overall conservation outcomes, not on species-specific prioritization.

Incorrect. There is no evidence in the texts to support the notion that conservation efforts for fireflies are inherently more effective than those for black bears. Both species are discussed in the context of collaborative conservation efforts, without a comparative analysis of effectiveness.

Question 259 Difficult
The correct answer is D.

D) Are the barriers used in Weremijewicz and colleagues' study sufficient to ensure that nutrient transfer could only occur via a CMN?
Correct. Option D best completes the question. Both Text 1 and Text 2 discuss the effectiveness of the barriers used in Weremijewicz and colleagues' study to isolate the Common Mycorrhizal Network (CMN) as the sole pathway for nutrient transfer. Text 1 explains the methodology of using barriers to exclude other pathways, while Text 2 critiques this approach by highlighting that the barriers also allow liquids to pass through, potentially enabling nutrient transfer through alternative pathways. Therefore, both texts would agree that questioning the sufficiency of the barriers to ensure exclusive nutrient transfer via the CMN is the most aligned question.
A) Do plants that transfer nutrients through a root-to-root pathway also transmit nutrients via a CMN?
Incorrect. This option asks whether plants use both root-to-root and CMN pathways for nutrient transfer. However, neither Text 1 nor Text 2 directly addresses whether both pathways are utilized simultaneously. Instead, they focus on the effectiveness of the barriers used to isolate the CMN pathway.
B) Is there an available barrier material that can block roots and liquids while allowing fungal strands through?
Incorrect. This option inquires about the availability of specific barrier materials that can selectively block roots and liquids while permitting fungal strands. While Text 2 touches on the permeability issues of the barriers, the main focus of both texts is not on the availability of such materials but rather on whether the existing barriers used in the studies were effective in isolating the CMN pathway.
C) Did Zhang and colleagues' study effectively exclude any nutrient pathway other than a CMN?
Incorrect. This option specifically targets the effectiveness of Zhang and colleagues' study in excluding other nutrient pathways besides the CMN. While Text 1 mentions that Zhang and colleagues did not use barriers to exclude other pathways, the question pertains only to their study. The correct option (D) encompasses the sufficiency of the barriers in Weremijewicz and colleagues' study, which both texts critique, making it the more comprehensive and aligned question.

Question 260 Difficult
The correct answer is D.

D) Is working a full-time job a major impediment to practicing the art of poetry?
Correct. Option D best captures a point of disagreement between Lorde (Text 1) and the author of Text 2.
Text 1 highlights Audre Lorde's view that poetry is accessible and can be practiced alongside other occupations. She emphasizes that poets can write "between shifts, in the hospital pantry, on the subway, and on scraps of surplus paper," suggesting that holding a full-time job does not significantly hinder one's ability to engage in poetry.
In contrast, Text 2 discusses the professionalization of poetry and points out that many successful poets have professional training and extensive practice, which requires considerable time. It argues that those who must maintain full-time jobs often lack the necessary time to dedicate to poetry, thereby indirectly limiting who can become poets. This implies that working a full-time job is a significant impediment to practicing poetry effectively.
A) Do other art forms than poetry require substantial investments of time and money?
Incorrect. Both texts acknowledge that other art forms often require more time and financial resources compared to poetry. Text 1 explicitly mentions that artists in fields like painting and architecture need "large blocks of uninterrupted time as well as money," which aligns with the general understanding supported by Text 2 regarding the demands of professional artistic endeavors.
B) Should poets try to gain experience working in nonartistic fields as a way of improving their poetry?
Incorrect. Neither text directly addresses whether poets should gain experience in nonartistic fields to enhance their poetry. Text 1 focuses on the accessibility of poetry alongside other jobs, while Text 2 discusses the impact of professionalization on who becomes a poet. Since the question of gaining experience in nonartistic fields is not explicitly covered or debated in either text, there is no basis for disagreement between the two authors on this matter.
C) Does professional training in poetry affect the quality of poets’ work?
Incorrect. While Text 2 mentions that many successful poets have professional training and extensive practice, it does not explicitly discuss the impact of this training on the quality of their work. Text 1 does not address professional training at all. Therefore, there is insufficient information to suggest that Lorde and the author of Text 2 would disagree on the role of professional training in determining the quality of poetry.
